following the date of publication in the Register of Regulations of a notice by the [name of person who would know when

the future action or event has occurred] that [insert future action or event].

Permit bill or bill Section to become effective when state agency is prepared for implementation

Section [number]. [This Act] [Section [number] of this Act] is effective immediately and is implemented on the

date of publication in the Register of Regulations of a notice of implementation by [Secretary/Other Highest Administrative

Officer/Board/Commission].

Delay implementation for specific amount of time or until date certain

Section [number]. [This Act] [Section [number] of this Act] is effective immediately and is implemented [{num-

ber} days, weeks, months, years after its enactment into law] [{month and day} following its enactment into law] [on

{month, day, and year}].

Contingent effective date to address a conflict or intersection with another bill

Section [number]. If [bill number] of the [number] General Assembly does pass both chambers of the General

Assembly by [date], Section [number] of this Act takes effect [date or number of days after the referenced bill] of the

[number] General Assembly takes effect.

Section [number]. If [bill number] of the [number] General Assembly does not pass both chambers of the General

Assembly by [date], Section [number] of this Act does not take effect.

OR

Section [number]. If [bill number] of the [number] General Assembly is enacted into law, Section [number] of

this Act takes effect [date or number of days after the referenced bill] of the [number] General Assembly takes effect.

Section [number]. If [bill number] of the [number] General Assembly is not enacted into law, Section [number]

of this Act does not take effect.

Sunset Clause

Section [number]. [This Act] [Section [number]; or, if necessary, citation for Delaware Code provision contained

in the Act] of this Act] expires [amount of time] after the Act’s enactment into law, unless extended by subsequent action

of the General Assembly.

Immunity

A [noun] who [action] under this section must do so in good faith and with reasonable care. Unless it is established

that the [noun] caused injuries or death as a result of unreasonable care, willfully, wantonly, or by gross negligence, a

[noun] is not subject to any of the following as a result of [action]:

  • (1) Disciplinary or other adverse action under the professional licensing laws of this State.
  • (2) Criminal liability.
  • (3) Civil liability for damages for injuries or death.

Civil Penalties

A [noun] who refuses, fails, or neglects to comply with [Code citation reference] is subject to a civil penalty as

follows:

  • (1) For a first occurrence, [specific amount or range of civil penalty], in addition to costs.
  • (2) For a second occurrence, [specific amount or range of civil penalty], in addition to costs.
  • (3) For a third or subsequent occurrence, [specific amount or range of civil penalty], in addition to costs.

Creating Crimes or Criminal Penalties

Create a crime

It is unlawful to [adverbial mens rea] [actus reus]. It is unlawful to knowingly push, shove, or otherwise touch another person in an offensive manner

Create criminal penalty when reference to the class of offense is sufficient to provide the penalty

Violation of [Code citation reference] is a [class of offense].

Create criminal penalty when a specific penalty, different from the general penalty for a class of offense is needed

Violation of [Code citation reference] is a [class of offense] punishable by [some specific mandatory or minimum

penalty within the range of the class of offense].

Violation of [Code citation reference] is punishable by [specific amount or range of fine or incarceration].

Citation to the Delaware Administrative Code, in the Delaware Code

Citation to a specific regulation as a whole

Regulation [number] of Title [number] of the Delaware Administrative Code

Regulation 1202 of Title 19 of the Delaware Administrative Code

Citation to a specific section of a specific regulation

Regulation [number of the regulation followed by a dash and the number of the specific section to be cited] of

Title [number] of the Delaware Administrative Code

Regulation 1202-18.0 of Title 19 of the Delaware Administrative Code Note: Lexis will translate the drafted citation to the Delaware Code into a citation to the “CDR” which is the Code of Delaware Regulations, a Lexis product, making the citation read, “CDR 19-1000-1202”. The correct way to draft these citations in a bill, however, are in the examples in this section.

Report required

  • (a) The [Department] shall annually prepare a report detailing [or including] all of the following:
  • (1) [Required information].
  • (2) [Required information].
  • (b) The [Department] shall submit the report to all of the following not later than [date] of each year:
  • (1) The President Pro Tempore and Secretary of the Senate, for distribution to all Senators.
  • (2) The Speaker and Chief Clerk of the House of Representatives, for distribution to all Representatives.
  • (3) The Director and Librarian of the Division of Legislative Services.
  • (4) The Director of the Delaware Public Archives.
  • (5) The Governor.

OR

The [Department] shall submit its annual report to the Governor, the General Assembly, and the Director and Librarian of the Division of Legislative Services no later than [date].

Resolution “Be it Resolved” Boilerplate

Note: Use the autopopulating language in DELIS by clicking the “Resolution Type” button. Based on the type of resolution selected on the “Draft Legislation” page, you should get the following:

Simple resolution

BE IT RESOLVED by the [House of Representatives or Senate] of the 151st General Assembly of the

State of Delaware that . . .

Concurrent resolution

BE IT RESOLVED by the [House of Representatives or Senate] of the 151st General Assembly of the

State of Delaware, the [Senate or House of Representatives] concurring therein, . . .

Joint resolution

BE IT RESOLVED by the [House of Representatives or Senate] and the [Senate or House of Represent-

atives] of the 151st General Assembly of the State of Delaware, with the approval of the Governor, that . . .

Synopsis

When making changes to existing law to conform to the Drafting Manual

This Act also makes technical corrections to conform existing law to the standards of the Delaware Legislative

Drafting Manual.

When including a delayed effective date, delayed implementation date, or a contingent effective date, include similar language in the synopsis

[This Act] [Section [number] of this Act] takes effect [number] [days, weeks, months, years] after its enactment

into law.

In a substitute to a bill, include the following to note what has changed:

House Substitute No. [number] for House Bill No. [number] differs from House Bill No. [number] as follows:

First leg of an amendment to the Delaware Constitution

This Act is the first leg of a constitutional amendment that would [brief explanation of amendment].

This Act requires a greater than majority vote for passage because § 1 of Article XVI of the Delaware Constitution

requires the affirmative vote of two-thirds of the members elected to each house of the General Assembly to amend the

Delaware Constitution.

Amending the Delaware Constitution requires not only the passing of the changes in this Act, but also passage of

the same changes after the next general election by the next General Assembly.

Second leg of an amendment to the Delaware Constitution

This Act is the second leg of a constitutional amendment that would [brief statement on what amendment does].

The first leg of this amendment, [bill number] of the [number] General Assembly, passed both Houses with the two-thirds

vote required by, and was publicized in accordance with, the Delaware Constitution.

Bill requiring a super-majority vote General language to tailor to specific situation

This Act requires a greater than majority vote for passage because [cite to applicable provision of the

Constitution] of the Delaware Constitution requires the affirmative vote of [the amount of the super-majority vote

required] of the members elected to each house of the General Assembly to [the action which requires the super-

majority vote].

Create a new crime or expand an existing crime that is within the criminal jurisdiction of an inferior court

This Act requires a greater than majority vote for passage because § 28 of Article IV of the Delaware

Constitution requires the affirmative vote of two-thirds of the members elected to each house of the General

Assembly to [create a new crime within the jurisdiction of the Court of Common Pleas, Family Court, or Justice

of the Peace Court] [expand the scope of an existing crime within the jurisdiction of the Court of Common Pleas,

Family Court, or Justice of the Peace Court]. Note: These are 2 different actions requiring a 2/3 vote, set off in this example by brackets.

Borrow money or create a debt

This Act requires a greater than majority vote for passage because § 3 of Article VIII of the Delaware

Constitution requires the affirmative vote of three-fourths of the members elected to each house of the General

Assembly to borrow money or create a debt by or on behalf of the State. Note: The exception to the requirement of using this language is when supplying casual deficiencies of revenue, repelling invasion, suppressing insurrection, defending the State in war, or paying existing debts.

Appropriate money to or issue or loan bonds of this State to a county, municipality, or cor-

poration, or pledge the credit of this State to guarantee or endorse the bonds of a county,

municipality, or corporation

This Act requires a greater than majority vote for passage because § 4 of Article VIII of the Delaware

Constitution requires the affirmative vote of three-fourths of the members elected to each house of the General

Assembly to [appropriate public money to a county, municipality, or corporation] [issue or loan bonds of this

State to a county, municipality, or corporation] [pledge the credit of this State to guarantee or endorse the bonds

or other undertakings of a county, municipality, or corporation]. Note: These are 3 different actions requiring a ¾ vote, set off here by bracketed phrases.

Appropriation, money not reverting

Section [number]. The General Assembly appropriates [dollar amount] from the General Fund in Fiscal

Year [number] to fund the [name of fund] created by the Act. Notwithstanding another law of this State to the

contrary, money in the [name of fund] that is unencumbered on [date], or [month, day] of a subsequent year,

does not revert to the State’s General Fund.

Note: Avoid specifying a dollar amount, if possible.

Increase existing tax or license fee

This Act requires a greater than majority vote for passage because § 10 of Article VIII of the Delaware

Constitution requires the affirmative vote of three-fifths of the members elected to each house of the General

Assembly to increase the effective rate of any tax levied or license fee imposed.

Create new tax or license fee

This Act requires a greater than majority vote for passage because § 11 of Article VIII of the Delaware

Constitution requires the affirmative vote of three-fifths of the members elected to each house of the General

Assembly to impose or levy a tax or license fee.

Amend general corporation law

This Act requires a greater than majority vote for passage because § 1 of Article IX of the Delaware

Constitution requires the affirmative vote of two-thirds of the members elected to each house of the General

Assembly to amend the general corporation law.

Amend municipal charter directly

This Act requires a greater than majority vote for passage because § 1 of Article IX of the Delaware

Constitution requires the affirmative vote of two-thirds of the members elected to each house of the General

Assembly to amend a municipal charter.

Amend municipal charter indirectly

This Act requires a greater than majority vote for passage because § 1 of Article IX of the Delaware

Constitution requires the affirmative vote of two-thirds of the members elected to each house of the General

Assembly to amend a municipal charter, whether directly, by amendment to a specific municipality’s charter, or,

as in this Act, indirectly, by a general law.

Amend Delaware Constitution

This Act requires a greater than majority vote for passage because § 1 of Article XVI of the Delaware

Constitution requires the affirmative vote of two-thirds of the members elected to each house of the General

Assembly to amend the Delaware Constitution.

Appendix A-17.1: Frequently Used Boilerplate Language for Provisions Common to Updating or Creating a Board, Commission, Council, or Similar Entity. 251

APPOINTMENTS WITH INPUT FROM NON-GOVERNMENTAL ENTITIES

The Governor shall appoint 2 members from the [name of organization]. The Governor may consider potential nominees from a list that [name of person in organization] provides.

COMPENSATION/REIMBURSEMENT

A member receives no compensation but may be reimbursed for the member’s actual and necessary expenses incurred in the performance of the member’s official duties.

OR

Each member of the Board must be reimbursed for all expenses involved in each meeting, including travel, and in addition must receive compensation per meeting attended in an ament determined by the Division under Section 9 of Article III of the Delaware Constitution.

NOTE: Senate confirmation for a Governor’s appointment is needed only if the salary exceeds $1500 per year. See Sec. 9 of Article III of the Delaware Constitution.

COUNCIL MAKEUP

The Board consists of 7 members who are appointed by the Governor and meet the following qualifications:

OR

The Advisory Council (“Council”) consists of 19 members. Membership is composed as follows:

  • (1) The Governor shall appoint 3 members of the public who are residents of this State, 1 from each county.
  • (2) The Mayor of the City of Wilmington.
  • (3) The Speaker of the House of Representatives shall appoint 3 members.
DESIGNEES

A member who serves on the Council by virtue of position may select a designee from the organization that the member represents to serve in their stead and at their pleasure. A member must provide the designation to the chair in writing. A designee has the same duties and rights as the member the designee represents.

NOTES: A Governor’s appointee may not send a designee. State if a member’s designee does not receive the compensation or reimbursement that the member would receive.

GEOGRAPHIC AND OTHER DIVERSITIES AS A PRIORITY

The Governor shall consider geographical and other diversities when appointing a member to the Council.

251 Specific entities or actors that are included in these boilerplate provisions are for example purposes only.

PARTY BALANCE

The Governor-appointed members must be balanced so that no more than [4] members are from 1 political party. An individual who declines to announce a political affiliation is eligible for appointment as a member of the Council.

NOTE: Remove reference to party balance if possible. Most boards, commissions, or councils do not need political balance.

POWERS

The Board may adopt procedural rules to carry out its functions under this chapter.

QUORUM

A majority of the [voting] members must be present at a Council meeting in order to have a quorum and conduct official business. A vacancy on the Board is not counted for quorum.

OR

A majority of the [voting] members must be present at a Council meeting in order to have a quorum and conduct official business. A majority of the [voting] members present is sufficient to approve an action of the Council.

OR

A majority of [voting] members constitutes a quorum for the purpose of transacting business. The Board may not take disciplinary action without the affirmative vote of at least 4 members.

NOTE: “Ex officio” does not mean “not voting.” An ex officio member can vote and therefore be counted for quorum unless the statute specifies otherwise.

REMOVAL FROM OFFICE

A member may be removed by the member’s appointing authority/The Governor may remove a member for gross inefficiency, misfeasance, nonfeasance, malfeasance, or neglect of duty in office.

  • (1) A member is deemed in neglect of duty if the member is absent from 3 consecutive Board meetings without good cause or fails to attend at least 50% of all regular Board meetings in a calendar year.
  • (2) A member who is deemed in neglect of duty is considered to have resigned.

OR

A member may be removed by the member’s appointing authority/The Governor may remove a member for gross inefficiency, misfeasance, nonfeasance, malfeasance, or neglect of duty in office. A member is deemed in neglect of duty if the member is absent from 3 consecutive, regular Board meetings or attends less than 50% of Board meetings in a calendar year. The Governor/member’s appointing authority may consider the member to have resigned and may accept the member’s resignation.

REPORTS

The Council shall submit its annual report to the Governor, the General Assembly, and the Director and the Librarian of the Division of Legislative Services not later than January 1.

SELECTING CHAIR

The Governor shall appoint the chair, who serves as chair at the pleasure of the Governor.

OR

The Board shall elect a chair from among its members.

OR

The Attorney General shall designate a chair from among the Council members.

NOTES: Use only 1 chair and 1 vice-chair, not 2 co-chairs, due to limitations in appointment computer system. If the Governor appoints the chair, the chair should serve at the Governor’s pleasure and be dismissed from the entire board if removed. The better practice is to have the board to elect the chair unless the board strongly wants the Governor to appoint the chair. If the position of an executive director is included in the board, commission, or council’s statute, the board, commission, or council should select or hire the executive director and put the hiring procedure in their bylaws. Do not give this authority solely to the chair. Address who supervises the executive director; the supervisor must be a state agency.

STAFFING

The Department, through the Secretary, shall provide reasonable staff support to assist the Board in performing its duties under this title.

STATEWIDE MEETINGS REQUIRED

At least 2 months before submitting the Plan required under this chapter, the Office shall hold a public meeting to allow public input regarding the Plan. The meeting must be held in all 3 counties in this State. The Office may hold separate meetings in each county or hold 1 meeting and, through the use of technology, provide the public with access to attend and participate in the meeting simultaneously from each of the 3 counties.

TERM LIMITS

Each member is appointed for a period of 3 years and may serve 1 additional term. Each term of office expires on the date specified in the appointment; however, a member remains eligible to participate in Board proceedings until the Governor replaces that member.

OR

Each member serves for a term of up to 3 years. The Governor may appoint a member for a term of less than 3 years to ensure terms expire on a staggered basis.

OR

A member serves until a replacement is appointed under the same process as the member's appointment.

OR

An individual who has served on the Board for 6 years may not be appointed to the Board again until the expiration of an interim period of at least 3 years since the individual was last served.

Appendix A-18: Background License Plate – Proceeds Benefit a Corporation, County, or Municipality

Appendix A-19: Background License Plate – Proceeds Benefit a State Entity or the General Fund

Appendix A-20: Organizational License Plate

Appendix B: Standard Form for a Resolution to Establish a Task Force (or other ad hoc entity)

NOTE: This form provides the standard language to use when drafting a resolution to establish a task force or other similar entity, along with notes on when to use the language and what to consider as you draft. Drafting notes are in blue, as are areas that should be replaced with the specific language for your resolution.

To continue a task force over General Assemblies, it must be reauthorized by an appropriate resolution at the start of a new two-year session of the second General Assembly. Similarly, a task force which is unable to meet its originally authorized reporting deadline must prepare an appropriate resolution for introduction in the Senate or House to extend the reporting deadline. A standard form to extend a task force over General Assemblies or a reporting deadline is provided in Appendix C.

The following standard form does not comply with the requirement to double space legislation. This is a conscious decision of the editor in order to save space. When drafting in conformity with this standard form, double space the legislation.

 House or Senate Simple Resolution  House or Senate Concurrent Resolution  House or Senate Joint Resolution (with the Governor)

ESTABLISHING THE [name] TASK FORCE TO STUDY AND MAKE FINDINGS AND RECOMMENDATIONS REGARDING [issue to be studied].

WHEREAS, ___________________________________; and WHEREAS, ____________________________;

NOW, THEREFORE:

 BE IT RESOLVED by the House of Representatives [by the Senate] of the [#] General Assembly of the State of Delaware that…

 BE IT RESOLVED by the House of Representatives [by the Senate] of the [#] General Assembly of the State of Delaware, the Senate [the House of Representatives] concurring therein, that…

 BE IT RESOLVED by the House of Representatives [the Senate] and the Senate [the House of Representatives] of the [#] General Assembly of the State of Delaware, with the approval of the Governor, that… reasons task force is needed, stated in whereas clauses

simple resolution

concurrent resolution

joint resolution …the [name] Task Force (“Task Force”) is established to study and make findings and recommendations regarding [same wording as in title].

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Task Force be comprised of the following [voting] members:

  • (1) [Two members of the House of Representatives, appointed by the Speaker of the House].
  • (2) [Two members of the Senate, appointed by the President Pro Tempore of the Senate].
  • (3) [A representative of the Department of Agriculture, appointed by the Secretary of the Department].
  • (4) [One citizen from each county, appointed by the Governor].

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Task Force be comprised of the following members:

  • (1) The following members serving by virtue of position, or a designee appointed by the member: a. [Member, often a cabinet secretary or legislator]. b. [Member, often a cabinet secretary or legislator].
  • (2) [Member not serving by virtue of position].
  • (3) [Create separate list for any non-voting members].

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that members serving by virtue of position who are granted the ability to designate another individual to attend a Task Force meeting must provide the designation in writing to the Chair. An individual attending a meeting for a member serving by virtue of position has the same duties and rights as the member serving by virtue of position.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the [Secretary of the Senate/Chief Clerk of the House] notify members of the Task Force serving by virtue of their position of selection to serve on the Task Force and notify individuals authorized to appoint members to the Task Force of their appointing authority.

create task force and state its purpose

list members when members are not serving by virtue of position

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that [designate by name or by position] is the chair of the Task Force.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the chair of the Task Force be responsible for guiding the administration of the Task Force by, at a minimum, doing all of the following:

  • (1) Notifying the individuals who are members of the Task Force and, if applicable, notifying a person of the need to nominate or appoint a member.
  • (2) Setting a date, time, and place for the initial organizational meeting.
  • (3) Supervising the preparation and distribution of Task Force meeting notices, agendas, minutes, correspondence, and reports.
  • (4) Sending to the President Pro Tempore of the Senate, the Speaker of the House of Representatives, and the Director of the Division of Legislative Services, after the first meeting of the Task Force, a list of the members of the Task Force and the person who appointed them.
  • (5) Providing meeting notices, agendas, and minutes to the Director of the Division of Legislative Services.
  • (6) Ensuring that the final report of the Task Force is submitted to the President Pro Tempore of the Senate and the Speaker of the House of Representatives, with copies to all members of the General Assembly, the Governor, the Director and the Librarian of the Division of Legislative Services, and the Delaware Public Archives.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that [name of the temporary chair, often the prime sponsor in the chamber where the resolution was introduced] serve as temporary chair to guide the initial organization of the task force by doing all of the following:

  • (1) Notifying the individuals of the formation of the Task Force and, if applicable, notifying a person of the need to nominate or appoint a member.
  • (2) Setting a date, time, and place for the initial organizational meeting.
  • (3) Supervising the preparation and distribution of the meeting notice and agenda for the initial organizational meeting of the Task Force.
  • (4) Providing the meeting notice and agenda for the initial organizational meeting to the Director of the Division of Legislative Services. if the chair is designated to notify members that the task force has been created, consider requiring the Secretary or Chief Clerk to notify the chair that the resolution has passed and provide the chair with a copy of the resolution

designate a chair

designating a chair is best practice; if not possible, use the following language

  • (5) Notifying the Director of the Division of Legislative Services of the individual chosen to be Chair of the Task Force and providing contact information for that individual.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the members of the Task Force choose a chair from among themselves at the initial organizational meeting.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that when a chair of the Task Force has been designated, the chair is responsible for guiding the administration of the Task Force by, at a minimum, doing all of the following:

  • (1) Supervising the preparation and distribution of meeting notices, agendas, minutes, correspondence, and reports of the Task Force.
  • (2) Sending to the President Pro Tempore of the Senate, the Speaker of the House of Representatives, and the Director of the Division of Legislative Services, after the first meeting of the Task Force, a list of the members of the Task Force and the person who appointed them, and providing notice of any changes in the make up of the Task Force to the President Pro Tempore of the Senate, the Speaker of the House of Representatives, and the Director of the Division of Legislative Services.
  • (3) Providing meeting notices, agendas, and minutes to the Director of the Division of Legislative Services.
  • (4) Ensuring that the final report of the Task Force is submitted to the President Pro Tempore of the Senate and the Speaker of the House of Representatives, with copies to all members of the General Assembly, the Governor, the Director and the Librarian of the Division of Legislative Services, and the Delaware Public Archives.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Task Force must hold its first meeting not later than [number of days after passage or enactment/specific date].

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Task Force study [purpose of study] and report its findings and recommendations.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that a quorum of the Task Force is a majority of its [voting] members. BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that:

  • (1) Official action by the Task Force, including making findings and recommendations, requires the approval of a quorum of the Task Force. set deadline by which the task force must meet for the first time; base the deadline on the number of day after passage or enactment or set a specific date

specify the mission and issues the sponsor wants specifically addressed

define quorum and provide the vote requirement for action and creation of procedural rules specify “voting members” if have non-voting members and wish to exclude them from quorum

  • (2) The Task Force may adopt rules necessary for its operation. If the Task Force does not adopt rules or if the adopted rules do not govern a given situation, Mason’s Manual of Legislative Procedure controls

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the [Senate caucus/House caucus/Division of Legislative Services/Controller General/other state agency] is responsible for providing reasonable and necessary support staff and materials for the Task Force.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the [chair/co-chairs] must compile a report containing a summary of the Task Force’s work regarding the issues assigned to it under this Resolution, including any findings and recommendations by, and submit the report to the General Assembly, the Governor, and the Director and the Librarian of the Division of Legislative Services not later than [specific date].

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that this [type of resolution] Resolution expires on the date the Task Force submits its findings and recommendations.

SYNOPSIS

This resolution establishes the [name] Task Force to [same wording as title]. Include historical information that may help explain the origination, purpose, and goals of the task force.

Appendix C: Standard Form for a Resolution Extending the Date by which the Findings and Recommendations Report of a Task Force is Due (or other ad hoc entity)

NOTE: This resolution should be the same type as the resolution that created the task force or similar ad hoc entity, and with the same sponsors, if possible. This form can be adapted to extend the life of an entity from one general assembly to the next.

EXTENDING THE REPORTING DATE OF THE [name] TASK FORCE.

WHEREAS, the [name] Task Force was established under [HR/SR/HCR/SCR/HJR/SJR] No. [#] of the [#] General Assembly to study [purpose of study] and to make findings and recommendations based on the study; and

WHEREAS, the Task Force was directed to submit its findings and recommendations report to [name of person] by [date]; and

WHEREAS, the Task Force has worked diligently on its study, but is not yet prepared to submit its report; and

WHEREAS, [sponsor’s reason for extension]; and

WHEREAS, the members of the Task Force believe that they will be able to submit the report by [date].

NOW, THEREFORE:

BE IT RESOLVED by the [same as in original resolution] that the date by which the findings and recommendations report of the [name] Task Force is due be extended to [date].

SYNOPSIS

This resolution extends the due date of the findings and recommendations report of the [name] Task Force from [date in original resolution] to [new date].

Appendix D: Important Delaware Cases for Bill Drafting

Legislative drafters should review the following Delaware cases, listed by subject matter.

Bill Title Requirements

Opinion of the Justices, 194 A.2d 855 (Del. 1963): A constitutional amendment’s bill title is not restrained by Section 16 of Article II. However, the bill title needs to give fair and reasonable notice to the public and the General Assembly of the bill’s subject matter.

Abrahams v. Superior Court of New Castle County 131 A.2d 662 (Del. 1957): A bill’s title can just reference the subject matter and area in the Code that will be changed.

State v. Hobson, 83 A.2d 846 (Del. 1951): A bill’s title need not include existing law being re-enacted in the bill.

Opinion of the Justices, 246 A.2d 90 (Del. 1968) A bill’s title need not be an index of its contents.

Binding; One General Assembly May Not Bind Another

News-Journal Co. v. Boulden, 1978 Del Ch LEXIS 536; Glassco v County Council of Sussex County, 1993 Del Ch LEXIS 34; Manigault v. Springs, 199 U.S. 473 (1905); Ohio Life Ins. & Trust Co. v Debolt, 57 U.S. 416 (1859); Opinion of the Justices of the Supreme Judicial Court of Maine, 673 A.2d 693 (Me. 1996): The current General Assembly cannot bind future General Assemblies or the House or Senate of a future General Assembly. No Delaware Supreme Court cases have been located.

Drafting Manual Cited in Case Law

City of Lewes v. Nepa, 212 A.3d 270 (Del. 2019): Citing the Delaware Legislative Drafting Manual in the Court’s explanation that “shall” and “may” is a “meaningful variation.”

Enactment Clauses & Constitutional Super-majority Vote Requirements

Opinion of the Justices, 276 A.2d 736 (Del. 1971): Two-thirds vote required if a bill even impliedly amends a municipal charter, not just charter change bills. See City of Newark v. Weldin, 1987 Del. Ch. LEXIS 539 (Del. Ch. 1987) (holding that, while an act of the General Assembly was a valid general act, because the act lacked a two-thirds vote the act could not impliedly repeal a conflicting portion of the Newark Charter).

Opinion of the Justices, 575 A.2d 1186 (Del. 1990): Three-fifths vote required for State to increase permit fees or license fees of any nature.

Opinion of the Justices, 249 A.2d 869 (Del. 1968): Three-fourths vote requirement is only for appropriations made to “any county, municipality or corporation.” See Del. Const. art VIII, § 4.

Brooks v. Taylor, 154 A.2d 386 (Del. 1959): The two-thirds vote requirement if a bill enlarges the criminal jurisdiction of an inferior court includes Family Court.

AETNA Casualty and Surety Company v. Smith, 131 A.2d 168 (Del. 1957): After finding the fire companies to be an arm of the county, the Supreme Court held that any appropriation of public money to a county, municipality, or corporation must receive a vote of three-fourths of the members elected to each branch of the legislature, whether that appropriation is direct or, as in this case, indirect.

State Agency as “Person”

State v. Del. Pub. Serv. Comm’n, 2020 Super. LEXIS 101, 2020 WL 888493 (Del. Super. Ct. 2020): The Delaware Department of Natural Resources and Environmental Control lacked standing to file an action seeking to declare regulations of the Delaware Public Service Commission because DNREC is not a “person” under the Administrative Procedures Act, Del. Code Ann. tit. 29, § 10141(a), and Del. Code Ann. tit. 1, § 302(15). The definition of “party” under Del. Code Ann. tit. § 10102(a) made clear that the General Assembly intended to distinguish a "person" from an "agency.”

State Offices and Officers

State ex rel. Craven v. Schorr, 131 A.2d 158 (Del. 1957): “Under our present Constitution the Governor of Delaware has no vested constitutional power to appoint to statutory offices. The decision to grant or withhold from him such power rests with the General Assembly.” Thus, it was permissible for the statute at issue to amend an existing statutory board by increasing its membership and designating the members in the legislation making the amendment.

State ex rel. James v. Schorr, 65 A.2d 810 (Del. 1948): The General Assembly can pass an act creating a state agency or board and name the members thereof in the act and authorize a state agency to make certain appointments; the General Assembly cannot delegate to a voluntary organization of individuals [a political party in this case] the power to appoint the members of a state agency; a statute expressly repealed in order to be replaced by a statute then declared unconstitutional is in fact held not to have been repealed.

New Castle County Council v. State, 688 A.2d 888 (Del. 1996): The General Assembly is afforded significant latitude when legislation restructures or modifies a statutory office and removal of incumbents is contingent and a necessary by-product of the restructuring or modification, the power of the General Assembly is limited only by Section 4 of Article XV of the Delaware Constitution (have to pay their emoluments of office).

State ex rel. Ward v. Churchman, 51 A. 49 (Del. 1902): Statutory construction of statutes and constitutions: “offices” and “officers” mean state or county “offices” or “officers” only, unless explicitly or by necessary implication extended to officers of municipal corporations.

In re: Request by the 138th General Assembly for an Advisory Opinion, 672 A.2d 4 (Del. 1996): Members of Title 23 and Title 24 boards or commissions are “public officers” for the purposes of holdover in Del. Const. art. XV, § 5; therefore, they can't be denied the right to participate in board or commission action, despite the expiration of their terms, until their successors are qualified to replace them.

Statutory Interpretation

State v. Dickerson, 298 A.2d 761 (Del. 1972): Section 308 of Title 1 grants a court the ability to uphold remaining provisions of a bill whenever a court strikes down part of a bill even when the bill itself does not include a severability section. But, see Rappa v. New Castle County, 18 F. 3rd 1043 (3rd Cir. 1994) (declined to follow because it would have resulted in greater free speech restrictions) and Evans v. State, 872 A.2d 539 (Del. 2005).

Wilmington Sav. Fund Soc. v. Green, 288 A.2d 273 (Del. 1972): Enrolled bill doctrine exception allows checking the vote count to determine if a bill received the constitutionally required number of votes for passage when the bill’s enactment clause requires a lesser amount.

State v. Hobson, 83 A.2d 846 (Del. 1951): When there are two possible ways to interpret a statute, the court is required to adopt the one which would uphold its validity.

Burpulis v. Director of Revenue, 498 A.2d 1082 (Del. 1985): If a literal interpretation leaves a result inconsistent with the general statutory intent, look to general intent.

Spielberg v. State, 558 A.2d 291 (Del. 1989): 1 Del. C. § 306 [Code section headings are not part of the Law] has only “historical significance” and does not foreclose questions of manifest intent of the General Assembly; where literal application of a statute yields an unreasonable or absurd result, don’t take it literally.

Daniels v. State, 538 A.2d 1104 (Del. 1988): “The unreasonableness of the result produced by one among alternative interpretations of a statute is just cause for rejecting that interpretation in favor of the interpretation that would produce a reasonable result.”

Wilmington Medical Center, Inc. v. Bradford, 382 A.2d 1338 (Del. 1978): If the question of the reasonable necessity for regulation is fairly debatable, legislative judgment must be allowed to control.

Sierra v. DSCYF, 2020 Del. Lexis 280 (Del. 2020): Statutes are presumed constitutional unless there is clear and convincing evidence of unconstitutionality.

Carper v. New Castle County Board of Education, 432 A.2d 1202 (Del. 1981): The synopsis is a “proper source from which to glean legislative intent.”

Board of Adjustment v. Verleysen, 36 A.3rd 326 (Del. 2012): “It is well established that a statutory synopsis cannot change the meaning of an unambiguous statute.”

Nigro v. Flinn, 192 A.2d 685 (Del. Super. Ct. 1937): The meaning and effect of 10 Del. C. § 8119 providing for limitation of actions in personal injury cases was not changed by its embodiment in the 1915 revision containing the present section; and thus action for injuries to infant brought on arriving at age twenty-one but fourteen years after injuries occurred was barred. Regarding the former 1-year statute of limitations found in 10 Del. C. § 8119.

City of Lewes v. Nepa, 212 A.3d 270 (Del. 2019): Citing the Delaware Legislative Drafting Manual in the Court’s explanation that “shall” and “may” is a “meaningful variation.”

Separation of Powers: Limits on Delegation of Legislative Power

In re School Code of 1919 (Opinion of the Chancellor and Judges of Delaware on the Request of the Governor), 108 A. 39 (1919): “If the existence of the law depends on the vote of the people or the will of one man even, it is an unconstitutional delegation of legislative power; but if the law is complete in and of itself, the fact that it provides for an acceptance of any of its provisions by certain states agencies does not make it a delegation of legislative power and, therefore, invalid.”

Separation of Powers: Limit on Power of the General Assembly

Evans v. State, 872 A.2d 539 (Del. 2005): The General Assembly cannot reverse a determination in a particular case, though it may prescribe a new rule for future cases; severability clause not usable when the Bill Title violates the single subject Constitutional mandate.

Appendix E-1: Check List – Bill Drafting

DELISError! Bookmark not defined. Draft Legislation Form  Does the bill create a report or an appointment to a board, commission, task force, etc.? If so, the “Creates Report” or “Creates Appointment” box on the DELIS draft legislation form must be checked. Caption Issues  Check that the correct prime sponsor is listed on the legislation.  Check those listed as sponsors against the list of those who agreed to sponsor, especially as to who are co-prime (and any agreed order). Formatting Issues  Mandatory Parts: Title; Enactment Clause; at least 1 Bill Section; Synopsis.  Enactment clause must begin “BE IT ENACTED” and if a preamble (whereas clauses) is included, the whereas clauses must precede the enactment clause. The initial creation of a bill includes an enactment clause, so any enactment clause above the whereas clauses must be deleted.  Check that the line numbers run down the left-hand side of the page on each page of the bill, but not beside the bill title, enactment clause, or synopsis.  All lines, except synopsis, must be double spaced.  “Track changes” formatting must be removed from “freeform” drafts.  All blank lines and pages must be removed.  If a Senate bill, it must have an “Author.”  Lists formatting requirements:  Introductory language states how many items required (ex. “1 or more,” “any,” or “all”).  First word of each item capitalized.  Each item ends with period. Prefatory Language Issues  The prefatory language must cite the amended portion of the Constitution, Code, or Laws of Delaware, and include “by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows.”  References to the portion of the Constitution (article), Code (title), or Laws of Delaware (volume and chapter) in the prefatory language must accurately reflect what is amended in the body of the bill. Underline and Strike Through Issues  Use strike through to reflect deleted text and underline to reflect inserted text.  If a bill Section deletes text, make sure the text exists where cited. After the deletion, the remaining text must still constitute a complete sentence.  If a bill Section inserts text, make sure that the inserted text makes sense where inserted. After the insertion, make sure the augmented text still constitutes a complete sentence. Drafting Manual Compliance Checks  Make sure the section heading for the Constitution or Code provision being amended or created is included after the prefatory language and before the provision being amended or created.  Make sure that the internal hierarchy of new and existing Code text complies with the Drafting Manual’s formatting requirements. See Drafting Rule 5.  When separating words in a series, use serial commas (Oxford commas). See Drafting Rule 23.  Check that “shall,” “must,” and “may” are used in compliance with Drafting Rules 11 and 12.  Do not use “shall not.” See Drafting Rule 12.  Do not use “and/or” and “including, but not limited to,” or related forms. See Drafting Rules 10(c) and 26(d).  Make sure tabulations are drafted in compliance with Drafting Rule 29.  Check that the bill complies with other requirements of the Drafting Manual not noted in this Checklist, especially the Formatting and Drafting Rules. Reference Checks  Do deletions or insertions impact internal references? If so, make corrections.  Check every reference, internal and external, for accuracy.  Search the Code for citations to the provision amended, to ensure changes in the provision do not have greater impact than intended or the sponsor is aware of the impact. Page 1 of 2

Conflict and Validity Checks  Perform a conflict check to determine if there is a conflict with already-introduced legislation.  Make sure the text in the bill is the current version of the law. Effective Date, Contingent Effective Date, and Sunset Date Checks  Check the effective date, contingent effective date, and sunset date. If it already passed or is shortly forthcoming is it intentional?  If there is no effective date or contingent effective date, is there a problem with the bill going into effect immediately?  If there are multiple effective dates, check that the entire bill is covered.  Check that the effective date, contingent effective date, and sunset date comply with the Drafting Manual’s requirements. Legal Issues  Check the bill title for compliance with the single subject rule, Sec. 16 of Art. II. See Drafting Manual, Part III, Ch. 2, Sec. 2, B.  Make sure the enactment clause has the proper majority required for passage (simple majority, 3/5, 2/3, or 3/4). See Drafting Manual, Part III, Ch. 2, Sec. 4, E.  Make sure the synopsis accurately describes what the title and body of the bill do. Final Checks  Double check the bill title for errors (Bill titles cannot be amended once introduced).  Make sure the bill title references each Code title, Constitution article, and provision of the Laws of Delaware that is amended in the bill body.  The synopsis must include boilerplate language if Drafting Manual required changes are made. See Drafting Manual, Part III, Ch. 2, Sec. 2, T.  Run spell check.  Have the bill proofread by someone not involved in its drafting. Notes to Drafter:

Appendix E-2: Check List – Amendment Drafting

DELIS Draft Legislation Form  Does the bill create a report or an appointment to a board, commission, task force, etc.? If so, the “Creates Report” or “Creates Appointment” box on the DELIS draft legislation form must be checked. Caption Issues  Mandatory Parts: At least 1 AMEND; Synopsis.  Check that the correct prime sponsor is listed on the legislation.  Check those listed against the list of those who agreed to sponsor, especially as to who are co-prime (and any agreed order). No co-sponsors from the other Chamber allowed. Formatting Issues  Check that line numbers run down the left-hand side of the page on each page of the amendment, but not beside the synopsis.  All lines, except synopsis, must be double spaced.  “Track changes” formatting must be removed from “freeform” drafts.  All blank lines and pages must be removed.  If a Senate amendment, it must have an “Author”. Amendment Instructional Language Issues  The first portion of an amendment to the legislation must begin “AMEND.” All additional portions in that amendment must begin “FURTHER AMEND.”  Check that the instructional language tells where in the legislation (what line number; before or after what word) text is to be deleted or inserted.  Check that quotation marks are at the beginning and end of text to be inserted into or deleted from the legislation.  Punctuation marks must be placed within the quoted text when intended to be included in the legislation, and outside the quoted text when intended to be part of the instructional language. Underline and Strike Through Issues  Use strike through to reflect deleted text and underline to reflect inserted text.  If an amendment deletes text, make sure the text exists where cited. After the deletion, the remaining text must still constitute a complete sentence.  If an amendment inserts text, the inserted text must make sense where inserted. After the insertion, the augmented text must still constitute a complete sentence. Drafting Manual Compliance Checks  Make sure the internal hierarchy of new and existing Code text added to the bill by the amendment complies with the Drafting Manual’s formatting requirements. See Formatting Rule 5, Delaware Legislative Drafting Manual (Drafting Manual).  When separating words in a series, use serial commas (Oxford commas). See Drafting Rule 23.  Use “shall”, “must”, and “may” in compliance with Drafting Rules 11 and 12.  Do not use “shall not.” See Drafting Rule 12.  Do not use “and/or” and “including, but not limited to,” or related forms. See Drafting Rules 10(c) and 26(d).  Make sure tabulations are drafted in compliance with Drafting Rule 29.  Check that the amendment complies with other requirements of the Drafting Manual not noted in this Checklist, especially the Formatting and Drafting Rules. Reference Checks  Do deletions or insertions by the amendment impact internal references? If so, make corrections.  Make sure every reference, internal and external, contained in the amendment has been checked for accuracy.  Search the Code for citations to the provision amended, to ensure changes in the provision do not have greater impact than intended or the sponsor is aware of the impact.

Page 1 of 2 220 Conflict and Validity Checks  Does the amendment seek to amend the same portion of the bill as another amendment? Can the amendment be re-drafted so as not to conflict with another amendment (unless conflict is the sponsor’s intent)?  Perform a conflict check to determine if the amendment conflicts with already-introduced legislation.  Are further amendments to the bill needed to account for any conflicting legislation that is already introduced or changes to the law since introduction of the bill?  Make sure the text in the bill and the amendment is still the current version of the law. Effective Date, Contingent Effective Date, and Sunset Date Checks  Check the effective date, contingent effective date, and sunset date. If it already passed or is shortly forthcoming, is that intentional?  If there is no effective date or contingent effective date, is there a problem with the bill going into effect immediately?  If there are multiple effective dates, check that the entire bill is covered.  Check that the effective date, contingent effective date, and sunset date comply with the Drafting Manual’s requirements. Legal Issues  Does the enactment clause for the bill being amended have the proper majority required for passage (simple majority, 3/5, 2/3, or 3/4) given any changes to the bill being made by the amendment?  Make sure the amendment falls within the single subject of the bill title. See Drafting Manual, Part III, Ch. 2, Sec. 2, B.  Make sure the synopsis accurately describes how the amendment has changed the bill. Final Checks  Make sure the amendment amends a portion of a Code title, Constitution article, or provision of the Laws of Delaware that is referenced in the bill title.  The synopsis must include boilerplate language if Drafting Manual required changes are made See Drafting Manual, Part III, Ch. 2, Sec. 2, T.  Run a spell check.  Have the amendment proofread by someone not involved in its drafting. Notes to Drafter:

Appendix E-3: Check List – Resolution Drafting

DELIS Draft Legislation Form  Does the bill create a report or an appointment to a board, commission, task force, etc.? If so, the “Creates Report” or “Creates Appointment” box on the DELIS draft legislation form must be checked. Caption Issues  Check that the correct prime sponsor is listed on the legislation.  Check those listed as sponsors against the list of those who agreed to sponsor, especially as to who are co-prime (and any agreed order). Formatting Issues  Mandatory Parts: Title; Preamble [Whereas’s are virtually mandatory]; at least 1 BE IT RESOLVED; Synopsis.  Make sure that if the resolution proclaims a day, week, or month, that the day, week, or month is set off using quotation marks.  Check that if the proclaimed day, week, or month ends the resolution title sentence, that the period is located outside of the quotation marks.  Make sure the inevitable preamble (whereas clauses) precedes the resolved clause.  Check that the line numbers run down the lefthand side of the page on each page of the resolution, but not beside the title or synopsis.  All lines, except synopsis, must be double spaced.  “Track changes” formatting must be removed from “freeform” drafts.  All blank lines and pages must be removed.  If a Senate resolution, must have an “Author”. Drafting Manual Compliance Checks Generally  When separating words in a series, use serial commas (Oxford commas). See Drafting Rule 23, Delaware Legislative Drafting Manual (Drafting Manual).  Use “shall,” “must,” and “may” in compliance with Drafting Rules 11 and 12, remembering that simple and concurrent resolutions are not law and so “shall” and “must” are not usable for them.  Do not use “shall not.” See Drafting Rule 12.  Do not use “and/or” and “including, but not limited to,” or related forms. See Drafting Rules 10(c) and 26(d).  Make sure tabulations are drafted in compliance with Drafting Rule 29.  Have the resolution proofread by someone not involved in its drafting.  Check that the resolution complies with other requirements of the Drafting Manual not noted in this Checklist, especially the Formatting and Drafting Rules. Drafting Manual Compliance Checks for Task Forces, Study Commissions and Similar Temporary Entities (“task force”)  Make sure that the resolution states the purpose of the task force.  If staff support will be required, make sure the resolution states who is responsible for providing it.  Make sure that the resolution provides who the members of the task force are or how members of the task force are appointed.  If the resolution provides for an individual serving by virtue of position to designate another to serve in their place, the resolution must require notice to the task force chair when such a designation takes place and what authority the designated individual has.  If the resolution grants authority to the Governor, President Pro Tempore, Speaker, Minority Leaders, or any other government official to name a person to serve on a task force, make sure the resolution uses the word “appoint” rather than “designate”, “name”, or another word.  Check that the resolution either designates a permanent chair or that it designates a temporary chair and provides for the selection of a permanent chair.

Page 1 of 2 222  Make sure the resolution includes a provision directing the chair to provide a list of the task force’s members, and the person who appointed them, to the President Pro Tempore, Speaker, and Director of the Division of Legislative Services following the task force’s first meeting.  Make sure the resolution includes provisions directing the chair to provide meeting notices, agendas, and meeting minutes, to the Director of the Division of Legislative Services.  Check that the resolution provides that a majority of members is necessary to constitute a quorum and take official action.  The resolution must grant the task force authority to adopt rules to govern its proceedings.  The resolution must set a deadline for the first meeting.  The resolution must set a date for any report that is required which gives the task force enough time to complete its work.  The resolution must include a provision requiring the task force to deliver a copy of any required report to the Director and the Librarian of the Division of Legislative Services.  The resolution must comply with Senate Rule 22 and House Rule 18.  Is the Drafting Manual’s standard task force language used in the resolution? If not, is the departure from the standard language reasonable? Effective Date, Contingent Effective Date, and Sunset Date Checks  Check the effective date, contingent effective date, and sunset date. If it already passed or is shortly forthcoming is that intentional?  If there is no effective date or contingent effective date, is there a problem with the resolution going into effect immediately?  If there are multiple effective dates, check that all provisions of entire resolution are covered.  Check that the effective date, contingent effective date, and sunset date complies with the Drafting Manual’s requirements. Legal Issues  If a simple or concurrent resolution, do not use language that directs or requires action. Simple and concurrent resolutions are not law and so cannot direct or require action.  If the resolution creates a task force, do not grant authority to appoint members of the task force to a nongovernmental entity or individual. Nongovernmental entities or individuals cannot appoint members of a task force.  Make sure the synopsis accurately describes what the title and body of the resolution do. Final Checks  Confirm that the caption, “BE IT RESOLVED” clause, and synopsis all reference the same resolution type (simple, concurrent, or joint).  Confirm that the language used in the resolution title, “BE IT RESOLVED” clause, and synopsis is consistent.  Double check all names and dates included in the resolution.  Run spell check.  Have the resolution proofread by someone not involved in its drafting. Appendix F: Interpretations of Statutes (Title 1, Chapter 3)

NOTE: This section has been added to provide an easy reference to a chapter of the Code that all drafters should read and reference.

TITLE 1. General Provisions.

CHAPTER 3. Interpretations of Statutes.

§ 301. Rules of construction and definitions. The rules of construction and the definitions set forth in this chapter shall be observed in the construction of this Code and all other statutes, unless such construction would be inconsistent with the manifest intent of the General Assembly, or repugnant to the Code or to the context of the same statute.

§ 302. Definitions. In the construction of this Code and of all other statutes of this State, unless the context requires a different meaning:

  • (1) “Adult” or “adult person” means a person of the age of 18 years or older.
  • (2) “Child” means a person who has not reached the age of 18 years.
  • (3) “Full age” means the age of 18 years or older.
  • (4) “Grantee” includes every person to whom a freehold estate or interest is conveyed.
  • (5) “Grantor” includes every person by whom a freehold estate or interest is conveyed.
  • (6) “Infancy” means an age of less than 18 years.
  • (7) “Infant” means a person who has not reached the age of 18 years.
  • (8) “Inhabitant” means a resident in any place.
  • (9) “Kin” and “kindred,” as applied to the descent of estates, signify kin or kindred by blood, and the degrees of consanguinity shall be computed by the civil law method; but collateral kindred claiming through a nearer common ancestor, shall be preferred to those claiming through a more remote common ancestor.
  • (10) “Lawful age” means the age of 18 years or older.
  • (11) “Minor” or “minor child” means a person who has not reached the age of 18 years.
  • (12) “Money” or “dollars” means lawful money of the United States.
  • (13) “Month” means a calendar month, unless otherwise expressed.
  • (14) “Oath” includes affirmation in all cases where an affirmation may be substituted for an oath, and "sworn" includes affirmed; and the forms shall be varied accordingly.
  • (15) “Person” and “whoever” respectively include corporations, companies, associations, firms, partnerships, societies and joint-stock companies, as well as individuals.
  • (16) “Person with a mental condition” includes every person with an emotional or psychiatric disorder or disability.
  • (17) “Real estate” or “real property” is synonymous with the phrase “lands, tenements and hereditaments.”
  • (18) “State” means the State of Delaware; and when applied to different parts of the United States, it includes the District of Columbia and the several territories and possessions of the United States.
  • (19) “Tavern” includes inn.
  • (20) “Under age” means an age of less than 18 years.
  • (21) “United States” includes its territories and possessions and the District of Columbia.
  • (22) “Will” means “last will and testament” and includes “codicil.”
  • (23) “Written” and “writing” respectively include printing and typewriting and reproductions of visual symbols by photographing, lithographing, multigraphing, mimeographing, manifolding or otherwise; but in all cases where the written signature of any person is by law required, it shall be the proper handwriting of such person, or if the person cannot write the person’s name, the person’s mark.
  • (24) “Year” means a calendar year, and is equivalent to the words “year of our Lord.”

§ 303. Words and phrases. Words and phrases shall be read with their context and shall be construed according to the common and approved usage of the English language. Technical words and phrases, and such others as may have acquired a peculiar and appropriate meaning in the law, shall be construed and understood according to such peculiar and appropriate meaning.

§ 304. Words of number and gender.

  • (a) Words used in the singular number include the plural and the plural includes the singular.
  • (b) Words importing the masculine gender include the feminine as well, except as otherwise clearly indicated by the context.
  • (c) All forms prescribed by law may be varied according to subsections (a) and (b) of this section.

§ 305. Classification and arrangement. The classification and organization of the titles, parts, chapters, subchapters, and sections of this Code, and the headings thereto, are made for the purpose of convenient reference and orderly arrangement, and no implication, inference or presumption of a legislative construction shall be drawn therefrom.

§ 306. Analyses of titles, parts, chapters, subchapters and sections; section headings; notes. The various analyses set out in this Code, constituting enumerations or lists of the titles, parts, chapters, subchapters and sections of this Code, and the descriptive headings or catchlines immediately preceding or within the texts of the individual sections of this Code, except the section numbers included in the headings or catchlines immediately preceding the texts of such sections, do not constitute part of the law. All derivation and other notes set out in this Code are given for the purpose of convenient reference, and do not constitute part of the law.

§ 307. References.

  • (a) Unless otherwise indicated in the context, references in this Code to titles, parts, chapters, subchapters or sections shall mean titles, parts, chapters, subchapters or sections of this Code.
  • (b) Whenever any reference is made to any portion of this Code or any other law, the reference applies to all amendments thereto.

§ 308. Severability of provisions. If any provision of this Code or amendments hereto, or the application thereof to any person, thing or circumstances is held invalid, such invalidity shall not affect the provisions or application of this Code or such amendments that can be given effect without the invalid provisions or application, and to this end the provisions of this Code and such amendments are declared to be severable.

Appendix G: Super-Majority Vote Requirements

CONSTITUTIONAL CITATION VOTE REQUIREMENT

three-fifths (3/5) of all the members elected to each house

Section 18 of Article III To override the Governor’s veto.

Section 6(c) of Article VIII To appropriate funds from the 2% “Rainy Day Fund” “in the event of emergencies involving the health, safety or welfare” of Delaware’s citizens.

Section 6(d) of Article VIII To appropriate from the Budget Reserve Account “such additional sums as may be necessary to fund any unanticipated deficit” or “to provide funds required as a result of any revenue reduction enacted by the General Assembly.” Section 10(a) of Article VIII To increase the effective rate “of any tax levied or license fee imposed by the State.” Section 11(a) of Article VIII To impose or levy a “tax or license fee.”

two-thirds (2/3) of all the members elected to each house

Section 9 of Article II To expel a member of the House by House members or to expel a member of the Senate by Senate members.

Section 19 of Article II To pass “laws relating to the laying out, opening, alteration or maintenance of any road or highway which forms a continuous road or highway extending through at least a portion of the three counties of the State.” Section 13 of Article III To “address” the Governor to request removal of an officer for reasonable cause.

Section 20(b) of Article III To determine “that the Governor is unable to discharge the powers and duties of his or her office because of mental or physical disability.” Section 1 of Article IV To establish additional courts (not judges). Section 12 of Article IV To require the entire Supreme Court to sit in a civil or criminal matter other than one in which the accused has been sentenced to death.

CONSTITUTIONAL CITATION VOTE REQUIREMENT

two-thirds (2/3) of all the members elected to each house (cont’d)

Section 28 of Article IV To give jurisdiction to inferior courts or justices of the peace of “such … misdemeanors as the General Assembly may from time to time … prescribe.” Section 1 of Article VI To impeach by the House; to convict by the Senate.

Section 1 of Article IX To enact or amend general incorporation laws and special acts of incorporation, including municipal charters. Section 1 of Article XVI To amend the Delaware Constitution, which requires passage by two consecutive General Assemblies. Section 2 of Article XVI To present to the voters the question of whether there should be a State constitutional convention.

three-fourths (3/4) of all the members elected to each house

Section 3 of Article VIII To borrow money or create a debt “by or on behalf of the State,” except “to supply casual deficiencies of revenue, repel invasion, suppress insurrection, defend the State in war, or pay existing debts.”

Section 4 of Article VIII To appropriate money to or issue or loan bonds of this State to any county, municipality or corporation; to pledge the credit of the State by guaranteeing or endorsing the bonds or other undertakings of any county, municipality or corporation. Section 12 of Article VIII To appropriate money in the Transportation Trust Fund for a purpose not listed in § 12(b).

Section 12 of Article VIII If money in the Transportation Trust Fund ceases to be appropriated for a purpose under § 12(b)(3), to again appropriate it for such a purpose.

The Delaware Transportation Authority may not issue bonds unless the General Assembly approves of the purpose for which the CONSTITUTIONAL CITATION VOTE REQUIREMENT 2 Del. C. § 1405(i) bonds are issued and the maximum amount of such bonds (noting, however, that since one General Assembly cannot bind a future General Assembly except by amending the Delaware Constitution, this vote requirement is advisory only: i.e., a future General Assembly, either explicitly or implicitly, could negate this “requirement” by a vote by a simple majority).
